1+\left(9+12\omega\right)a^{-2}x

\,\,\,\,+\left[a^{4}+\left(103+92\omega\right)a^{-4}-\left(10+12\omega\right)\right]x^{2}

\,\,\,\,+\left[\left(567+588\omega\right)a^{-6}-\left(204+200\omega\right)a^{-2}\right]x^{3}+\ldots

(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}
(\mathrm{Dih}_{8}^{[0]})_{\langle r,s\rangle}
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s}
[r]
or
[r^{3}]
(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}

Figure 5
:
The
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{4}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of the affine
D_{4}
quiver and gauging of various subgroups. Here
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s}
denotes the two-group formed by the one-form symmetry that is dual to
(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}
and the zero-form symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
.

Let us now study such possible gaugings in more detail.

•

Let us consider theory (
3.1
) with
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
zero-form symmetry, whose symmetry category is 2-Vec
(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
. If we gauge
(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}
, we land on theory (
3.1
) wreathed by the normal Klein (NK) subgroup of
S_{4}
. The corresponding symmetry category is 2-Vec
(\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s})
where
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s}
is the two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s}
formed by the dual
(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[1]})_{\widehat{r}}
one-form symmetry and the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
zero-form symmetry. Using the index, this can be shown as follows:

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}}(a|\omega|s=1;x)\\
&\overset{(\ref{FpDih8})}{=}\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=0,\ldots,3}{\cal F}_{p}(r=e^{\frac{2\pi ij}{4}},s=1)x^{p}\\
&\overset{(\ref{systemDih8})}{=}\frac{1}{4}\left[{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega;x)+3\times{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}(a|\omega;x)\right]\\
&\overset{(\ref{IndexNKwr})}{=}{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\text{NK}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indNKwr})~,\end{split}

(3.103)

where we set
s=1
and, in the third line, we used the following properties:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.91}{$\begin{split}&{\cal F}_{p}(r=e^{\frac{\pi i}{2}},s=1)={\cal F}_{p}(r=e^{\frac{3\pi i}{2}},s=1)={\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4})}}(a|\omega;x)~,\\
&{\cal F}_{p}(r=e^{\pi i},s=1)\overset{(\ref{systemDih8})}{=}3\times{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}(a|\omega;x)-2\times{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4})}}(a|\omega;x)~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.104)

Note that, in this case, the wreathing group, namely the normal Klein subgroup of
S_{4}
, does not coincide with the group that we gauge, namely
(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}
. This is due to the mixed anomaly (
3.68
), which makes the Klein four-group
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
extended to
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
.
16
16

16

This is completely analogous to the discussion in
(
52
, Footnote 20)
in the following way. In
(2+1)
dimensions, we can go from the
\mathrm{Spin}(6)
gauge theory, which has a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[1]}
one-form symmetry, to the
\mathrm{SO}(6)/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
gauge theory, which has a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]}
zero-form magnetic symmetry, in two steps. The first one is to gauge a
\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[1]}
to obtain the
\mathrm{SO}(6)
gauge theory, which has a
\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}
one-form symmetry and a dual
\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetry. The second step is to gauge
\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}
of the
\mathrm{SO}(6)
gauge theory to obtain the
\mathrm{SO}(6)/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
gauge theory. In the latter, the original
\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetry of the
\mathrm{SO}(6)
gauge theory, together with the dual
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form symmetry arising from gauging, forms a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
zero-form magnetic symmetry.

Moreover, if we turn on the fugacity
s
, we can further gauge the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
symmetry and we reach theory
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
.

•

On the other hand, if we gauge
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
first in (
3.102
), we obtain an index, whose explicit expression reads

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}}(a|\omega|r=1;x)\\
&=1+\left(6+6\omega\right)a^{-2}x+\left[\left(62+70\omega\right)a^{-4}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-2a^{4}}-\left(7+6\omega\right)\right]x^{2}\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left[3a^{2}+\left(467+446\omega\right)a^{-6}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-3a^{6}}-\left(157+160\omega\right)a^{-2}\right]x^{3}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.105)

If we take the Higgs branch limit of the index as described in (
2.39
), i.e. we read the coefficients of the terms contributing as
a^{2p}x^{p}
, with
p\geq 0
, we see that the Higgs branch Hilbert series contains negative terms, which are highlighted in
red
in the index above. Given that such terms cannot be associated with gauge invariant operators parametrising the Higgs branch, we refer to (
3.105
) as an
invalid index
.

The reason for this invalid index is similar to that described around (
3.90
). The normaliser of
H=(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}=\langle S\rangle
in
K=\mathrm{Dih}_{8}\cong(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{4})_{r}\rtimes(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}=\langle R,S\rangle
is the Klein four-group
N(H,K)=\{1,S,R^{2},R^{2}S\}
. Therefore, after gauging
H
, we are left with the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
global symmetry described by
(
55
, (1.4))

\begin{split}N(H,K)/H\cong\{1,R^{2}\}~.\end{split}

(3.106)

Upon gauging the symmetry associated with
s
in (
3.102
) by summing it over
\pm 1
and dividing by two, we are left with the fugacities
r
and
r^{3}
, but these correspond to the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry which is not really there after gauging
H
. Upon setting them to unity, we then obtain the invalid index. For example, the term
-2a^{4}x^{2}
, highlighted in red in (
3.105
), originates from
[(1+r^{2})-2(r+r^{3})]a^{4}x^{2}
in (
3.102
) with
r
set to
1
.

Another point of view on this is as follows. Gauging the non-normal subgroup
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}
in
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
makes the whole symmetry become non-invertible, described by

\begin{split}\text{2-}\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[1]}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\text{2-}\mathrm{Rep}[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\cong\text{2-}\mathrm{Rep}(\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s})~,\end{split}

(3.107)

as pointed out in
(
15
, (5.83))
and
(
16
, (3.64))
.

The invalid index, due to gauging
H
in such a naive manner, as discussed above, reflects the presence of this non-invertible symmetry. We will shortly describe the procedure of getting a valid index in (
3.108
).

Nevertheless, if we turn on the fugacity
r
in (
3.105
) and gauge the corresponding
(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}
symmetry, we reach the wreathed quiver
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
.

•

Gauging the two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s}
.
Starting from theory
(\ref{affineD4})/(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}
, whose symmetry category is 2-Vec
(\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s})
, we can reach the theory associated with the 2-Rep
(\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s})
symmetry category by gauging the two-group. This can be achieved at the level of the index by gauging the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
symmetry in the index of
(\ref{affineD4})/(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}
first and, subsequently, restore all of the terms containing
either

[r]=\{r^{2},r,rs\}

or

[r^{3}]=\{r^{2},r^{3},r^{3}s\}
.
17
17

17

The reason for this can be understood by considering the map (
3.112
), in which case
S
acts by swapping
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
, see (
3.111
). Using this parametrisation,
[r]
and
[r^{3}]
coincide respectively with
[x_{1}]=\{x_{1},x_{1}s,x_{1}x_{2}\}
and
[x_{2}]=\{x_{2},x_{2}s,x_{1}x_{2}\}
, where
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
denote the fugacities associated with
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
. Since, in our case,
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
has been gauged, the dual
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{s}}
one-form symmetry can no longer exchange
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
(see the reason in Footnote
15
), meaning that we can restore all of the terms containing
either

[x_{1}]

or

[x_{2}]
, but not both, in the index.
For instance, if we choose the former option, we obtain a valid index for theory
[(\ref{affineD4})/(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}]/\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r},s}
, whose expression reads

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{[\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}]/\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r}s}}(a|\omega|[r];x)\\
&=1+\left[3+6\omega+\left(3+4\omega\right)\left(r^{2}+rs\right)-2\omega r\right]a^{-2}x\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left\{\left[1+r^{2}+rs-2r\right]a^{4}+\left[39+30\omega\right.\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left.\left.\left(35+28\omega\right)\left(r^{2}+rs\right)+\left(-6+6\omega\right)r\right]a^{-4}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,-\left.\left[4+6\omega+\left(3+4\omega\right)\left(r^{2}+rs\right)-2\omega r\right]\right\}x^{2}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.108)

Upon setting
[r]=\{r^{2},r,rs\}
to unity, we obtain the index of (
3.1
) wreathed by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
:

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{[\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}]/\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r}s}}(a|\omega|[r]=1;x)={\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indZ2wr})~.\end{split}

(3.109)

This is in agreement with
[\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}]/\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{r}s}\cong\eqref{affineD4}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

As a final step, starting from (
3.108
), we can further sum
rs
and
r^{2}
over
\pm 1
and sum
r
over the four fourth roots of unity. Doing so, we recover the index of
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
given by (
3.98
), as expected.

3.7.2
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
as a semi-direct product
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}

Instead of (
3.93
), we can alternatively represent the dihedral group of order eight as

\begin{split}\mathrm{Dih}_{8}=\left\{1,X_{1}S,X_{1}X_{2},X_{2}S,S,X_{1},X_{1}X_{2}S,X_{2}\right\}~,\end{split}

(3.110)

with

\begin{split}S^{2}=X_{1}^{2}=X_{2}^{2}=1,\quad X_{1}X_{2}=X_{2}X_{1},\quad SX_{1}S=X_{2}~.\end{split}

(3.111)

To recover (
3.93
), we can set

\begin{split}X_{1}=RS\quad\text{and}\quad X_{2}=SR=R^{3}S~.\end{split}

(3.112)

The group
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
can be realised as a semi-direct product
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}\cong(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
characterised by the following
split
exact sequence:

\begin{split}1\,\longrightarrow\,\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,\longrightarrow\,\mathrm{Dih}_{8}\,\longrightarrow\,\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,\longrightarrow\,1~.\end{split}

(3.113)

We denote the zero-form symmetry corresponding to the subgroup
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
of
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
as
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}
, where each factor is generated by
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
respectively. We denote by
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
the zero-form symmetry associated with the last
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, generated by
S
, in the above exact sequence. As can be seen from the last equality of (
3.111
),
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
swaps
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}
and
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}
by conjugation.

Let us consider the index of theory (
3.1
) refined with respect to the fugacities
s
,
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
associated with the elements
S
,
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
in (
3.111
) respectively. It can be derived by using the fugacity maps
x_{1}=rs
and
x_{2}=r^{3}s
in (
3.102
). The required result is

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.93}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|s,\{y,z\};x)\\
&=1+\left[3+6\omega+\omega\left(s+yzs\right)+\left(3+4\omega\right)\left(yz+y+z\right)-2\omega\left(ys+zs\right)\right]a^{-2}x\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left\{\left[1+s+yzs+yz+y+z-2\left(ys+zs\right)\right]a^{4}+\left[39+30\omega\right.\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\left.+\left.\left(12+9\omega\right)\left(s+yzs\right)+\left(35+28\omega\right)\left(yz+y+z\right)+\left(-6+6\omega\right)\left(ys+zs\right)\right]a^{-4}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\left.-\left[4+6\omega+\omega\left(s+yzs\right)+\left(3+4\omega\right)\left(yz+y+z\right)-2\omega\left(ys+zs\right)\right]\right\}x^{2}+\ldots~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.114)

where, for the sake of conciseness, we write
y=x_{1}
and
z=x_{2}
. This index satisfies

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|s,\{y,z\};x)={\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|s,\{z,y\};x)~,\end{split}

(3.115)

namely it is invariant under swapping
y\leftrightarrow z
. Upon gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}
, the resulting theory possesses a dual
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}
one-form symmetry and its index is given by

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/{(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}}}(a|\omega|s,\{y,\cdot\};x)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{z=\pm 1}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|s,\{y,z\};x)~,\end{split}

(3.116)

where we put a dot in the curly brackets to indicate that, since
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}
is gauged, the corresponding fugacity disappears from the index. If we now gauge the symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}
of theory
{\cal T}\equiv(\ref{affineD4})/{(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}}
, at the level of the index we obtain a similar result to
(
29
, (A.12))
:

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{{\cal T}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}]}(a|\omega|s,\{\cdot,z\};x)&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{y=\pm 1}{\cal I}_{{\cal T}/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}}(a|\omega|s,\{y,z\};x)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{y=\pm 1}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|s,\{y,z\};x)\\
&\overset{(\ref{swapsymm})}{=}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{y=\pm 1}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|s,\{z,y\};x)\\
&={\cal I}_{{\cal T}}(a|\omega|s,\{z,\cdot\};x)~,\end{split}

(3.117)

where, in the first line, we used the fact that, upon gauging the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}
symmetry of theory
{\cal T}
, the fugacity associated with the dual
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}
symmetry is restored in the index. As pointed out in
(
29
, Appendix A)
, this means that theory
{\cal T}
is self-dual under gauging its
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}
symmetry, which implies that the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
symmetry of theory (
3.1
) becomes a non-invertible symmetry in theory
{\cal T}
. The corresponding non-invertible defect implements the self-duality property
[
56
,
53
]
and defines the non-invertible symmetry forming the fusion two-category 2-Rep
[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{1}}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}\rtimes(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}]

[
15
,
16
]
.
18
18

18

We can see the origin of the notation
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{1}}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}\rtimes(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
as follows. Note that we can form a topological interface between the dual theories
{\cal T}
and
{\cal T}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}]
, where the Dirichlet boundary condition is imposed on the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}
gauge field. Since the two theories are dual to each other, this creates a topological defect in the single theory
{\cal T}
. In theory
{\cal T}
we have the symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}
, whereas in theory
{\cal T}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{2}}}]
we have the symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{1}}}
. As we move across the topological interface, these two symmetries get interchanged by the action of the non-invertible symmetry that arises from
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
. This therefore explains the aforementioned notation.
Note that we have encountered this symmetry in (
3.107
).

:
The
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}\cong(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}

•

Starting from (
3.114
), if we gauge the whole normal subgroup
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{1}}
of
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
, we obtain the index for the normal Klein wreathing of (
3.1
), in a very similar fashion to (
3.103
) where the
(\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{[0]})_{r}
normal subgroup of
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
is gauged. The result is shown in the box marked by the symmetry 2-Vec
((\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})^{2}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})
.

Upon further gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{s}
, we obtain the quiver (
3.1
) wreathed by
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
, whose symmetry is 2-Rep
(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
, as required.

•

On the other hand, if we start from (
3.114
) and gauge only
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{1}}
, we have the dual one-form symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2})_{\widehat{x}_{1}}
and the zero-form symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{2}}
with
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}
trying to exchange
X_{1}
in the one-form symmetry with
X_{2}
in the zero-form symmetry. This suggests that
[(\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2})_{\widehat{x}_{1}}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{2}}]\rtimes(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}
forms a two-group, denoted by
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{x}_{1},x_{2},s}
, such that its ’t Hooft anomaly is characterised by
\alpha\in H^{4}(\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{x}_{1},x_{2},s},\U(1))
. The correponding symmetry is denoted by 2-Vec
α
\{[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x_{1}}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}]\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]}\}
. The result is similar to that discussed around (
3.75
)–(
3.78
), and we shall not repeat the argument here. As before, we denote the global symmetry in this case by
\mathfrak{su}(4)*
. We emphasise again that if we compute the difference between this index and that of the quiver wreathed by the non-normal Klein subgroup of
S_{4}

(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{NNK}
given by (
3.34
), we obtain precisely (
3.78
), which indicates the ’t Hooft anomaly of the two-group symmetry in question.

•

Let us now move to the left part of Figure
6
. Starting from (
3.114
) and gauging
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}
, we expect to get an
invalid
index, as discussed in (
3.89
) and (
3.105
), for the reason provided around (
3.106
). Note that here we use a different parametrization in the index (
3.114
) from that described in (
3.102
), and so it is natural to expect to see the different invalidity.

Indeed, we see that gauging
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}
leads to two extra moment map operators associated with the term
2\omega
at order
x
, and so the global symmetry is
\mathfrak{so}(8)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)
. This is because the terms with negative sign at order
x
in (
3.114
) are removed. This seems to be physically unreasonable since the global symmetry gets larger than the original theory (
3.1
), which is
\mathfrak{so}(8)
, after gauging
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}
. For this reason, we put
\dagger
to denote this feature. The invalid index, obtained by gauing
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{s}
naively in this way, reflects the presence of the non-invertible symmetry (
3.107
). In fact, we remind the readers that the valid index for
\text{2-Rep}[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})^{2}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]}]
has already been obtained in (
3.108
) by means of gauging the appropriate two-group.

Nevertheless, we can press on and further gauge
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{1}}
. This gets rid of the term
(6+6\omega)
at order
x
. This amounts to removing a 12-dimensional representation from the right-hand side of the branching rule (
3.75
). This can indeed be achieved by removing the terms
(\mathbf{6})(2)\oplus(\mathbf{6})(-2)
. Furthermore, we need to remove the term
(156+144\omega)-(74+58\omega)=82+86\omega
at order
a^{-4}x^{2}
. However, we cannot remove a 168-dimensional representation of
\mathfrak{su}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)
out of the irreducible representations on the right-hand side of (
3.76
), since the equation

\begin{split}&84x_{\mathbf{84}}+64(2x_{\mathbf{64}})+20(3x_{\mathbf{20^{\prime}}})+15x_{\mathbf{15}}+6(2x_{\mathbf{6}})+x_{\mathbf{1}}=168~,\\
&\qquad\qquad\text{with}~0\leq x_{i}\leq 1,\end{split}

(3.118)

does not have a non-negative integer solution.
19
19

19

We can consider instead the equation
84x_{\mathbf{84}}+64x_{\mathbf{64}}+20x_{\mathbf{20^{\prime}}}+15x_{\mathbf{15}}+6x_{\mathbf{6}}+x_{\mathbf{1}}=168
, with
0\leq x_{\mathbf{84}}\leq 1,~0\leq x_{\mathbf{64}}\leq 2,~0\leq x_{\mathbf{20^{\prime}}}\leq 3,~0\leq x_{\mathbf{15}}\leq 1,~0\leq x_{\mathbf{6}}\leq 2,~0\leq x_{\mathbf{1}}\leq 1
, and find two non-negative integer solutions: (i)
x_{\mathbf{64}}=x_{\mathbf{20^{\prime}}}=2
and the others
x_{i}=0
, and (ii)
x_{\mathbf{84}}=x_{\mathbf{64}}=x_{\mathbf{20^{\prime}}}=1
and the others
x_{i}=0
. In this case, we need to take the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
charge into account, since the symmetry of the resulting theory is
\mathfrak{su}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)
. In solution (ii), there is no reason why we should remove one of the
\mathbf{64}(\pm 2)
representations but not the others. In solution (i), there is no reason why we should remove two of the
\mathbf{20^{\prime}}
representations but not the other.

Due to the inconsistency with this branching rule, we thus denote the global symmetry of the gauged theory by
\mathfrak{su}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)**
.

Moreover, the difference between the index of (
3.1
) wreathed by the double transposition subgroup of
S_{4}
, given by (
3.26b
), and the index in the box labelled by
\mathfrak{su}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)**
is precisely equal to (
3.78
). This seems to indicate an ’t Hooft anomaly of the two-group formed by the two dual symmetries
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{x}_{1}}
,
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{s}}
, and the zero-form symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}
.

Finally, if we further gauge
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{x_{2}}
, we arrive at
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
as required.

3.7.3
Gauging the centre
\langle R^{2}\rangle
of
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
and the Type III anomaly

The dihedral group
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
of order eight has a centre
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
generated by
R^{2}
. In other words,
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})=\langle R^{2}\rangle\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.
The quotient of
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
by
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
is the inner automorphism group:
\mathrm{Inn}(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})\cong\mathrm{Dih}_{8}/Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
by
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
. We can describe
\mathrm{Dih}_{8}
as a non-trivial extension of
\mathrm{Inn}(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
by
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
, characterised by

\begin{split}0\,\longrightarrow\,Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})\,\longrightarrow\,\mathrm{Dih}_{8}\,\longrightarrow\,\mathrm{Inn}(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})\,\longrightarrow\,0~.\end{split}

(3.119)

Explicitly,
\mathrm{Inn}(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
is generated by
\sigma_{R}
and
\sigma_{S}
, with the action given by
\sigma_{g}(a)=gag^{-1}
. Its element can be viewed in terms of the corresponding cosets of the centre, whereby the identity element of
\mathrm{Inn}(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
corresponds to
\{1,R^{2}\}
. We can identify
\sigma_{R}
with
\{R,R^{3}\}
,
\sigma_{S}
with
\{S,R^{2}S\}
, and
\sigma_{R}\sigma_{S}=\sigma_{S}\sigma_{R}
with
\{RS,R^{3}S\}
.

As pointed out in
[
17
]
,
(
15
, (5.5))
and
[
29
]
, gauging the zero-form symmetry
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
leads to a mixed ’t Hooft anomaly between the one-form symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]}
dual to
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
,
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{R}}
and
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{S}}
. Let
B^{(2)}
,
A^{(1)}_{\sigma_{R}}
and
A^{(1)}_{\sigma_{S}}
be the background fields for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]}
,
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{R}}
and
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{S}}
respectively. The aforementioned mixed anomaly is sometimes referred to as the Type III anomaly in the literature, and is characterised by (see e.g.
(
57
, (3.5))
,
(
53
, (D3))
,
(
16
, (2.55))
and
(
29
, (B.7))
)

\begin{split}\int_{M_{4}}B^{(2)}\cup A^{(1)}_{\sigma_{R}}\cup A^{(1)}_{\sigma_{S}}~,\end{split}

(3.120)

where
M_{4}
is a four manifold whose boundary is the three dimensional spacetime in which our theory lives. In this section, we examine the manifestation of this anomaly in the index.

In fact, we have seen a similar situation for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
in (
3.62
). In the same way as in (
3.63
), we parametrise the fugacity
r
associated with the group element
R
by

\begin{split}r=\exp\left[{\frac{2\pi i}{4}(\mathfrak{q}+2\mathfrak{n})}\right]~,\quad\text{with}\quad\mathfrak{q}=0,1~,\quad\mathfrak{n}=0,1~,\end{split}

(3.121)

where
\mathfrak{n}
is the variable associated with
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
, and
\mathfrak{q}
is associated with
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{R}}
.

Gauging
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
in the index

Let us first gauge the centre
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
by substituting (
3.121
) into (
3.102
), summing
\mathfrak{n}
over
0
and
1
, and dividing by two. The result is

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.99}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})}(a|\omega|\mathfrak{q},s;x)\\
&={\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}}}(a|\omega|\mathfrak{q};x)~\text{given by}~(\ref{IndexmodZ2nfy})\\
&\quad+(2\mathfrak{y}s\omega)a^{-2}x+\left[(-12\mathfrak{y}+24\mathfrak{y}s-16\mathfrak{y}\omega+18\mathfrak{y}s\omega)a^{-4}+2\mathfrak{y}sa^{4}-2\mathfrak{y}s\omega\right]x^{2}\\
&\quad+\left[(-144\mathfrak{y}+150\mathfrak{y}s-138\mathfrak{y}\omega+168\mathfrak{y}s\omega)a^{-6}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\left.+(42\mathfrak{y}-48\mathfrak{y}s+40\mathfrak{y}\omega-40\mathfrak{y}s\omega)a^{-2}-2\mathfrak{y}sa^{2}+2\mathfrak{y}sa^{6}\right]x^{3}+\ldots~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.122)

where, as before, we define the fugacity
\mathfrak{y}
as

\begin{split}\mathfrak{y}\equiv\frac{1+(-1)^{\mathfrak{q}}}{2}~.\end{split}

(3.123)

We emphasise that the fugacity
s
always appears together with
\mathfrak{y}
as the combination
\mathfrak{y}s
in the index. Upon turning on the background field for
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{R}}
, which amounts to setting
\mathfrak{q}=1
(i.e.
\mathfrak{y}=0
), the fugacity
s
disappears completely from the index. This indicates that the operators in the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{R}}
-twisted sector of theory
\eqref{affineD4}/Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
transform trivially under
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{s}}
.

Let us report the above index with
\mathfrak{q}=1
(i.e.
\mathfrak{y}=0
) and
s=\pm 1
:

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})}(a|\omega|\mathfrak{q}=1,s=\pm 1;x)\\
&=1+2\omega a^{-2}x+[(4+2\omega)a^{-4}-(1+2\omega)]x^{2}\\
&\qquad+[(3+6\omega)a^{-6}-3a^{-2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-a^{6}}+a^{2}]x^{3}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.124)

Note that this is an
invalid index
for the same reason as discussed above, namely the negative term highlighted in
red
gives rise to a negative term in the Higgs branch Hilbert series when the Higgs branch limit is taken.

It is also interesting to consider the index (
3.122
) when
\mathfrak{q}=0
(i.e.
\mathfrak{y}=1
), namely the background field associated with
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{R}}
is turned off. We report the result with
s=1
below:

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})}(a|\omega|\mathfrak{q}=0,s=1;x)\\
&=1+\left(6+12\omega\right)a^{-2}x+[(98+76\omega)a^{-4}+4a^{4}-(7+12\omega)]x^{2}\\
&\qquad+[(485+536\omega)a^{-6}-(175+160\omega)a^{2}-3a^{2}+3a^{6}]x^{3}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.125)

Here we see that the moment map operators indicate that the global symmetry is
[\mathfrak{su}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)]\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)
, where from (
3.122
) we see the
\mathfrak{su}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)
comes from (
3.69
) and the last two
\mathfrak{u}(1)
factors come from the term
2\mathfrak{y}s\omega
.

Note that, if we start from
{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})}(a|\omega|\mathfrak{q}=0,s;x)
and gauge the symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\sigma_{S}}
by summing
s
over
\pm 1
and dividing by
2
, we obtain precisely the index indicated in Figure
6
labelled by
\mathfrak{su}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)**
. The notation
**
indicates the inconsistency with the branching rule discussed around (
3.118
). We encounter this situation again due to the mixed ’t Hooft anomaly (
3.120
). We point out again that the difference between the index of (
3.1
) wreathed by the double transposition given by (
3.11
) and the index of (
3.1
)
/Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})/(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{S}}
is precisely equal to (
3.78
). As before, this indicates the ’t Hooft anomaly of the two-group formed by the one-form symmetries, that are dual to
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
and
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{S}}
, and the zero-form symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{R}}
.

Summing
(\ref{gaugecentreq1})
and
(\ref{gaugecentreq0})
and dividing by 2, which amounts to gauging
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
and
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{R}}
, we obtain theory (
3.1
) wreathed by the normal Klein subgroup of
S_{4}
, i.e.
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{NK}
, whose index is depicted in Figure
6
labelled by
\mathfrak{usp}(4)
.

Gauging
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{R}}
in the index

On the other hand, we can consider gauging
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{R}}
by substituting (
3.121
) into (
3.102
), summing
\mathfrak{q}
over
0
and
1
, and dividing by two. As a result, we have

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{R}}}(a|\omega|\mathfrak{n},s;x)\\
&=1+\left[(3-3s+6\mathfrak{z}s)+(8-4\mathfrak{z}-3s+8\mathfrak{z}s)\omega\right]a^{-2}x\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left\{\left[(45-12\mathfrak{z}-23s+70\mathfrak{z}s)+(24+12\mathfrak{z}-19s+56\mathfrak{z}s)\omega\right]a^{-4}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.+\left[(-4+3s-6\mathfrak{z}s)+(-8+4\mathfrak{z}+3s-8\mathfrak{z}s)\omega\right]+(3-4\mathfrak{z}+2\mathfrak{z}s)a^{4}\right\}x^{2}\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left\{\left[(103+132\mathfrak{z}-91s+332\mathfrak{z}s)+(148+78\mathfrak{z}-97s+362\mathfrak{z}s)\omega\right]a^{-6}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left.\left[(-50-30\mathfrak{z}+38s-124\mathfrak{z}s)+(-40-40\mathfrak{z}+40s-120\mathfrak{z}s)\omega\right]a^{-2}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.+(-2+4\mathfrak{z}-2\mathfrak{z}s)a^{2}+(2-4\mathfrak{z}+2\mathfrak{z}s)a^{6}\right\}x^{3}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(3.126)

where, as before, we define the fugacity
\mathfrak{z}
as

\begin{split}\mathfrak{z}\equiv\frac{1+(-1)^{\mathfrak{n}}}{2}~.\end{split}

(3.127)

We have the following results:

•

Upon setting
\mathfrak{n}=0
(i.e.
\mathfrak{z}=1
) in (
3.126
), we obtain the index depicted in Figure
6
labelled by
\mathfrak{su}(4)*
. This is the same result as gauging theory (
3.1
) by
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{1}}
or
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{2}}
.

•

Starting from (
3.126
), further gauging
Z(\mathrm{Dih}_{8})
by summing
\mathfrak{n}
over
0
and
1
, and setting
s=1
, we obtain the index of
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{NK}
, depicted in Figure
6
labelled by
\mathfrak{usp}(4)
. As expected, this is the same result as gauging theory (
3.1
) by
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{1}}
and
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{x_{2}}
.

•

However, starting from (
3.126
), further gauging
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{2})_{\sigma_{S}}
by summing
s
over
\pm 1
, and setting
\mathfrak{n}=0
(i.e.
\mathfrak{z}=1
), we obtain an
invalid
index

\begin{split}1+(3+4\omega)a^{-2}x+[(33+36\omega)a^{-4}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-a^{4}}-(4+4\omega)]x^{2}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(3.128)

where the
red
term gives rise to a negative term in the Higgs branch Hilbert series upon taking the Higgs branch limit.

3.8
A_{4}
wreathing of the affine
D_{4}
quiver

We can now examine the global zero-form
A_{4}\subset S_{4}
symmetry of the affine
D_{4}
quiver (
3.1
), where
A_{4}
is the alternating group on four objects defined as

\begin{split}A_{4}=\left\{1,Q,Q^{2},M,N,MN,MQ,NQ,MQ^{2},NQ^{2},MNQ,MNQ^{2}\right\}~.\end{split}

(3.129)

with

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.97}{$Q^{3}=M^{2}=N^{2}=1,~QMQ^{2}=MN=NM,~QNQ^{2}=M~,$}\end{split}

(3.130)

It can be realised as a semi-direct product
A_{4}\cong(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{3}
characterised by the
split
exact sequence:

\begin{split}1\,\longrightarrow\,\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,\longrightarrow\,A_{4}\,\longrightarrow\,\mathbb{Z}_{3}\,\longrightarrow\,1~.\end{split}

(3.131)

We denote the zero-form symmetry associated with the normal subgroup
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
by
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
, where

m
and
n
are the fugacities associated
M
and
N
such that
m^{2}=n^{2}=1
, and denote by
(\mathbb{Z}^{[0]}_{3})_{q}
the zero-form symmetry associated with
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
in the exact sequence generated by
Q
. The corresponding fugacity
q
is such that
q^{3}=1
.

The theory arising from the
A_{4}
wreathing of the affine
D_{4}
quiver (
3.1
) can be derived as

\begin{split}(\ref{affineD4})/{A_{4}}=&\frac{1}{12}\left[(\ref{affineD4})+(\ref{affineD4})_{(12)(34)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(13)(24)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(14)(23)}\right.\\
&\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left.(\ref{affineD4})_{(123)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(132)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(134)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(243)}\right.\\
&\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left.(\ref{affineD4})_{(234)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(124)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(142)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(143)}\right]~,\end{split}

(3.132)

where
(\ref{affineD4})_{(12)(34)}
,
(\ref{affineD4})_{(13)(24)}
and
(\ref{affineD4})_{(14)(23)}
take the form (
3.9
), whereas the eight terms of type
(\ref{affineD4})_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}
are given by (
3.12
). Explicitly, the expressions for the associated indices are given by (
3.11
) and (
3.14
) respectively, from which we obtain

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.96}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}(a|\omega;x)\\
&=1+\left(3+5\omega\right)a^{-2}x+\left[\left(27+21\omega\right)a^{-4}-\left(4+5\omega\right)\right]x^{2}\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left[a^{6}+a^{2}+\left(106+117\omega\right)a^{-6}-\left(43+40\omega\right)a^{-2}\right]x^{3}\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left[a^{8}+18+24\omega+\left(459+437\omega\right)a^{-8}-a^{4}-\left(278+285\omega\right)a^{-4}\right]x^{4}+\ldots~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.133)

whose Higgs and Coulomb branch limits (
2.39
) reproduce the results in
(
10
, Figure 9 and Figure 11)
.

The index of theory (
3.1
) can be refined with the fugacities
m
,
n
and
q
by considering the follwing ansatz:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.91}{$\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|m,n,q;x)&={\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}(a|\omega;x)+\left(m+n+mn\right)\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}y_{1,p}x^{p}\\
&+\left(q+q^{2}+mq+nq+mq^{2}+nq^{2}+mnq+mnq^{2}\right)\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}y_{2,p}x^{p}~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.134)

which must satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}(a|\omega;x)&=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{m,n=\pm 1}\,\sum_{j=0,1,2}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|m,n,q=e^{\frac{2\pi ij}{3}};x)=(\ref{indmodA4})~,\\
{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega;x)&={\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|m=n=q=1;x)=(\ref{indaffineD4})~,\end{split}

(3.135)

where
y_{1,p}\equiv y_{1,p}(a,\omega)
and
y_{2,p}\equiv y_{2,p}(a,\omega)
are two sets of unknown variables. We remark that the fugacities associated with the elements of the same cycle structure appear with the same set of unknowns in the ansatz (
3.134
), i.e. the fugacities appearing with
y_{1,p}
correspond to the elements
(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})
, whereas the fugacities appearing with
y_{2,p}
are associated with the elements
(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})
. From (
3.132
), the unknowns
y_{1,p}
and
y_{2,p}
can be determined by solving the following system of equations:

\begin{split}\left[{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indaffineD4})\right]_{x^{p}}=&{\cal F}_{p}(m=n=q=1)~,\\
\left[{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indZ2(12)(34)})\right]_{x^{p}}=&\frac{1}{3}\Big[{\cal F}_{p}(m=-1,n=q=1)\\
&\quad+{\cal F}_{p}(n=-1,m=q=1)\\
&\quad+{\cal F}_{p}(m=n=-1,=q=1)\Big]~,\\
\left[{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indZ3(123)})\right]_{x^{p}}=&\frac{1}{8}\sum_{m,n\pm 1}\,\sum_{j=1,2}{\cal F}_{p}(m,n,q=e^{\frac{2\pi ij}{3}})~,\end{split}

(3.136)

where
[{\cal I}]_{x^{p}}
is the coefficient of
x^{p}
in the series expansion of
{\cal I}
and we define

\begin{split}{\cal F}_{p}(m,n,q)&=\left[{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indmodA4})\right]_{x^{p}}+\left(m+n+mn\right)y_{1,p}\\
&+\left(q+q^{2}+mq+nq+mq^{2}+nq^{2}+mnq+mnq^{2}\right)y_{2,p}~.\end{split}

(3.137)

We therefore find the following series expansion of the index of (
3.1
) refined with the fugacities
m
,
n
and
q
:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.91}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega|m,n,q;x)\\
&=1+\left[3+5\omega+\left(3+\omega\right)\left(m+n+mn\right)\right.\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.+\omega\left(q+q^{2}+mq+nq+mq^{2}+nq^{2}+mnq+mnq^{2}\right)\right]a^{-2}x\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left\{\left[-2\left(m+n+mn\right)+\left(q+q^{2}+mq+nq+mq^{2}+nq^{2}+mnq+mnq^{2}\right)\right]a^{4}\right.\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.+\left[27+21\omega+\left(11+17\omega\right)\left(m+n+mn\right)\right.\right.\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.+\left.\left(12+9\omega\right)\left(q+q^{2}+mq+nq+mq^{2}+nq^{2}+mnq+mnq^{2}\right)\right]a^{-4}\right.\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.-\left[4+5\omega+\left(3+\omega\right)\left(m+n+mn\right)\right.\right.\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.+\left.\omega\left(q+q^{2}+mq+nq+mq^{2}+nq^{2}+mnq+mnq^{2}\right)\right]\right\}x^{2}+\ldots~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.138)

3.8.1
Sequentially gauging subgroups of
A_{4}

Starting from the above expression of the index, we can now sequentially gauge the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}
,
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
and
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
symmetries associated with the fugacities
m
,
n
and
q
in various ways, as summarised in Figure
7
. Let us discuss the following options.

1.

We can first gauge the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
symmetry and, subsequently, we can gauge the remaining
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
symmetry to arrive at theory
(\ref{affineD4})/{A_{4}}
.

2.

We can also reach theory
(\ref{affineD4})/{A_{4}}
by first gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
and, subsequently, gauging the remaining
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
symmetry.

3.

If we gauge only the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
subgroup of the
A_{4}
zero-form symmetry, the theory possesses a dual
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{m}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{n}}
one-form symmetry. This forms a two-group with the
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
zero-form symmetry. We will see that, upon gauging such a two-group symmetry, we obtain the wreathed quiver
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathbb{Z}_{3}
.

Let us now analyse in detail the various options presented in Figure
7
.

•

Given theory (
3.1
) with the global
A_{4}
zero-form symmetry, whose symmetry category is 2-Vec
(A_{4})
, we can gauge
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}
, or, equivalently,
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
, and we land on theory
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{DT}
, with associated symmetry category 2-Vec
[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{m}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}\rtimes(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}]
. At the level of the index, this can be shown as follows:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.95}{$\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}}(a|\omega|n=q=1;x)&\overset{(\ref{indexaffD4mnq})+(\ref{Fmnq})}{=}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=\pm 1}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}{\cal F}_{p}(m,n=q=1)x^{p}\\
&\overset{(\ref{systemA4})}{=}\frac{1}{2}\left[{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}}}(a|\omega;x)+{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(12)(34)}}}(a|\omega;x)\right]\\
&\overset{(\ref{IndexDTwr})}{=}{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathrm{DT}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indZ2wrDT})~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.139)

where we set
m=q=1
and, in the second line, we used the fact that

\begin{split}{\cal F}_{p}(m=-1,n=q=1)&={\cal F}_{p}(n=-1,m=q=1)\\
&={\cal F}_{p}(m=n=-1,q=1)~.\end{split}

(3.140)

After gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}
, if we turn on the fugacity
n
in (
3.139
), then also
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
can be gauged. This amounts to gauging the zero-form
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}\subset A_{4}
symmetry of theory (
3.1
), which, at the level of the index, can be performed as

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]}(a|\omega|q=1;x)\\
&\overset{(\ref{indexaffD4mnq})+(\ref{Fmnq})}{=}\frac{1}{4}\sum_{m,n=\pm 1}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}{\cal F}_{p}(m,n,q=1)x^{p}\\
&\overset{(\ref{systemA4})}{=}\frac{1}{4}\left[{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}}}(a|\omega;x)+3\times{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)\right]\\
&\overset{(\ref{IndexNKwr})}{=}{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathrm{NK}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indNKwr})~.\end{split}

(3.141)

This shows that, upon gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
, the resulting theory is
(\ref{affineD4})/\mathrm{NK}
, with symmetry category 2-Vec
(\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{m},\widehat{n},q})
, where
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{m},\widehat{n},q}
is the two-group formed by the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{m}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{n}}
one-form symmetry and the
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
zero-form symmetry. Finally, if we turn on the fugacity
q
in (
3.141
) and we further gauge the remaining
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
symmetry, we arrive at the wreathed quiver
(\ref{affineD4})/A_{4}
.

•

On the other hand, gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
first in theory (
3.1
) leads to an
invalid
index, in the same way as discussed around (
3.89
) and (
3.105
), whose explicit expression reads

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}}(a|\omega|m=n=1;x)=(\ref{indexA4})/{(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}}\big|_{m=n=1}\\
&=1+\left(12+8\omega\right)a^{-2}x+\left[\left(60+72\omega\right)a^{-4}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-6a^{4}}-\left(13+8\omega\right)\right]x^{2}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(3.142)

where the term highlighted in
red
signals that the Higgs branch limit admits a series expansion containing negative terms, which cannot describe gauge invariant quantities parametrising the Higgs branch of theory
\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
.

The invalidity of the index follows from the argument presented in
(
55
, (1.4))
which is also described around (
3.90
). Indeed, the normaliser of
H=(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
in
K=A_{4}
is
N(H,K)=(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
, meaning that the quotient
N(H,K)/H
is trivial. However, if we naively gauge the
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
symmetry, we are left with the fugacities
m
and
n
associated with the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}
and
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
symmetries respectively, but these are not really there after gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
. As a consequence, upon setting such fugacities to unity, we obtain the invalid index. The interpretation is that, after gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
, the whole symmetry becomes non-invertible and is described by
\text{2-Rep}(\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{m},\widehat{n},q})=\text{2-Rep}\{[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{m}}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]})_{\widehat{n}}]\rtimes(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}\}
. Therefore, the invalid index, obtained by naively gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
, signals the presence of such non-invertible symmetry. We will describe another way, namely gauging the two-group, that leads to a valid index below.

The same problem persists if we turn on the fugacity
m
(resp.
n
) in (
3.142
) and we further gauge
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}
(resp.
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
) in theory
\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
, for which the index reads

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{[\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}]/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}}(a|\omega|n=1;x)=[(\ref{indexA4})/{(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}}]/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\big|_{n=1}\\
&=1+\left(6+6\omega\right)a^{-2}x+\left[\left(38+38\omega\right)a^{-4}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-2a^{4}}-\left(7+6\omega\right)\right]x^{2}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.143)

Nonetheless, we can turn on the remaining fugacity
n
(resp.
m
) in (
3.143
) and gauge the associated
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}
(resp.
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}
) symmetry, from which we reach the wreathed quiver
(\ref{affineD4})/A_{4}
.

•

Gauging the two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{m},\widehat{n},q}
.
Let us start from the index of theory
(\ref{affineD4})/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]
, whose symmetry category is 2-Vec
(\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{m},\widehat{n},q})
, and let us gauge the two-group as follows. First, we gauge
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
in the index of theory
\eqref{affineD4}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]
and, subsequently, we restore all of the terms which appear with a combination of discrete fugacities containing
only one
among
m
,
n
, or their diagonal combination
mn
. More explicitly, these three options amount to restore all fugacities contained in either
[m]
,
[n]
, or
[mn]
, where

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.97}{$[m]\equiv\left\{m,mq,mq^{2}\right\}~,~[n]\equiv\left\{n,nq,nq^{2}\right\}~,~[mn]\equiv\left\{mn,mnq,mnq^{2}\right\}~.$}\end{split}

(3.144)

For definiteness, let us start with theory
\eqref{affineD4}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]
, then gauge
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}
and, subsequently, restore only the terms appearing with
[m]
. From (
3.134
), the required index is of the form

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}(a|\omega;x)+m\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}y_{1,p}x^{p}+\left(mq+mq^{2}\right)\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}y_{2,p}x^{p}~,\end{split}

(3.145)

with
y_{1,p}
and
y_{2,p}
solutions of (
3.136
). Explicitly, we obtain

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\{\eqref{affineD4}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]\}/\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{m},\widehat{n},q}}(a|\omega|[m];x)\\
&=1+\left[3+5\omega+\left(3+\omega\right)m+\omega\left(mq+mq^{2}\right)\right]a^{-2}x\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left\{\left[-2m+\left(mq+mq^{2}\right)\right]a^{4}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.+\left[27+21\omega+\left(11+17\omega\right)m+\left(12+9\omega\right)\left(mq+mq^{2}\right)\right]a^{-4}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.-\left[4+5\omega+\left(3+\omega\right)m+\omega\left(mq+mq^{2}\right)\right]\right\}x^{2}~.\end{split}

(3.146)

Since there is no longer a zero-form symmetry which swap
M
,
N
and
MN
among them, we cannot restore terms appearing with two or more combinations among
[m]
,
[n]
, or
[mn]
.
20
20

20

From (
3.130
),
Q
swaps
M
with
MN
,
MN
with
N
and
N
with
M
by conjugation. Gauging
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{q}=\langle Q\rangle
leads to the dual one-form symmetry
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[1]})_{\widehat{q}}
, but the latter can no longer act on the elements of the zero-form symmetry by conjugation as discussed in Footnote
15
, due to the fact that the symmetry defect of the one-form symmetry is of higher codimension than that of the zero-form symmetry.
The theory arising from this procedure of gauging the two-group turns out to be equivalent to the theory arising from the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
wreathing of (
3.1
). Indeed, at the level of the index, the following equality holds:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.99}{${\cal I}_{\{\eqref{affineD4}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]\}/\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{m},\widehat{n},q}}(a|\omega|[m]=1;x)={\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indZ3wr})~,$}\end{split}

(3.147)

where the notation
[m]=1
means that we set
m
,
mq
and
mq^{2}
to unity. The validity of this equality can be shown as follows. First, observe that, given
y_{1,p}
and
y_{2,p}
solutions of (
3.136
), we have

\begin{split}&m\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}y_{1,p}x^{p}+\left(mq+mq^{2}\right)\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}y_{2,p}x^{p}\\
&={\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}}(a|\omega|m,q;x)-{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]}(a|\omega|q;x)~,\end{split}

(3.148)

where (
3.139
) and (
3.141
) imply that

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}}(a|\omega|m=q=1;x)-{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]}(a|\omega|q=1;x)\\
&={\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathrm{DT}}}(a|\omega;x)-{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathrm{NK}}}(a|\omega;x)~,\end{split}

(3.149)

meaning that the expression (
3.145
) for
[m]=1
can be written as

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\{\eqref{affineD4}/[(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{m}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{n}]\}/\Gamma^{(2)}_{\widehat{m},\widehat{n},q}}(a|\omega|[m]=1;x)\\
&={\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}}(a|\omega;x)+{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathrm{DT}}}(a|\omega;x)-{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathrm{NK}}}(a|\omega;x)~.\end{split}

(3.150)

Next, we can use (
3.25
) and (
3.42
) to rewrite (
3.150
) in the form

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.99}{$\begin{split}(\ref{gaugeZ2mZ2nGamma})&={\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}}(a|\omega;x)+\frac{1}{2}\left[{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}}}(a|\omega;x)+{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\frac{1}{4}\left[{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}}}(a|\omega;x)+3{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)\right]\\
&={\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}}(a|\omega;x)+\frac{1}{4}\left[{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}}}(a|\omega;x)-{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)\right]~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.151)

Finally, using (
3.132
), we can replace
{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)
in the above expression with the following linear combination of indices:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.96}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)\\
&=\frac{1}{3}\times\left[12\times{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/A_{4}}(a|\omega;x)-{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega;x)-8\times{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}}}(a|\omega;x)\right]~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.152)

Doing so, we end up with

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.95}{$(\ref{gaugeZ2mZ2nGamma})=\frac{1}{3}\left[{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|\omega;x)+2\times{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}}}(a|\omega;x)\right]\overset{(\ref{IndexZ3wr})}{=}{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}/\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}(a|\omega;x)~,$}\end{split}

(3.153)

which is precisely the equality (
3.147
).

Starting from (
3.146
), we can further sum
mq
and
mq^{2}
over the three cube roots of unity and sum
m
over
\pm 1
, from which we obtain the index of
(\ref{affineD4})/A_{4}
given by (
3.133
).

3.9
S_{4}
wreathing of the affine
D_{4}
quiver

Hitherto, we have discussed wreathing of theory (
3.1
) by every subgroup of
S_{4}
, up to automorphisms. Let us now analyse the wreathing by the group
S_{4}
itself. This is given by a sum over 24 terms, each of them corresponding to an element of
S_{4}
, divided by the order of the group, i.e. 24, namely

\begin{split}(\ref{affineD4})/{S_{4}}=&\frac{1}{24}\left[(\ref{affineD4})+(\ref{affineD4})_{(12)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(13)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(14)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(23)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(24)}\right.\\
&\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left.(\ref{affineD4})_{(34)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(12)(34)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(13)(24)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(14)(23)}\right.\\
&\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left.(\ref{affineD4})_{(123)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(124)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(132)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(134)}\right.\\
&\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left.(\ref{affineD4})_{(142)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(143)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(234)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(243)}\right.\\
&\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left.(\ref{affineD4})_{(1234)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(1243)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(1324)}\right.\\
&\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\left.(\ref{affineD4})_{(1342)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(1423)}+(\ref{affineD4})_{(1432)}\right]~,\end{split}

(3.154)

where the six terms
(\ref{affineD4})_{(i_{1}i_{2})}
are of the form (
3.6
), the three terms
(\ref{affineD4})_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}
are given by (
3.9
), the eight terms
(\ref{affineD4})_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}
take the form (
3.12
) and, finally, the six terms
(\ref{affineD4})_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4})}
can be depicted as (
3.15
). Explicitly, their indices are given by

\begin{split}\begin{array}[]{ll}{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indZ2(12)})~,&\quad{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2})(i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indZ2(12)(34)})~,\\
{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{indZ3(123)})~,&\quad{\cal I}_{{\eqref{affineD4}_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4})}}}(a|\omega;x)=(\ref{ind(1234)})~,\end{array}\end{split}

(3.155)

whereas the index of (
3.1
) is given by (
3.5
). Using (
3.154
), we can compute the index of theory
(\ref{affineD4})/S_{4}
, which possesses 2-Rep
(S_{4})
symmetry. This reads

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/S_{4}}(a|\omega;x)\\
&=1+\left(3+5\omega\right)a^{-2}x+\left[\left(27+21\omega\right)a^{-4}-\left(4+5\omega\right)\right]x^{2}\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left[a^{2}+\left(100+110\omega\right)a^{-6}-\left(42+40\omega\right)a^{-2}\right]x^{3}\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left[a^{8}+17+24\omega+\left(397+374\omega\right)a^{-8}-\left(241+246\omega\right)a^{-4}\right]x^{4}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.156)

Note that the Coulomb branch moment map operators, which contribute to the term
a^{-2}x
in the above index, indicate that the flavour symmetry of the wreathed quiver
(\ref{affineD4})/S_{4}
is
\mathfrak{su}(3)
. Taking the Coulomb and Higgs branch limits of the index
(\ref{indmodS4})|_{\omega=1}
, we obtain the same results as the ones reported in
(
10
, Figures 9 and 11)
.

3.10
Summary: semi-direct products versus non-trivial extensions

We have discussed several examples concerning gauging symmetries involved in a semi-direct product and those involved in a non-trivial central extension of groups. Let us summarise the features of the indices in each case.

Let
{\cal T}
be a theory of our interest with a continuous non-Abelian symmetry
{\cal X}
, and a zero-form discrete finite symmetry
G
that is manifest in a certain quiver description of
{\cal T}
. Here
G
or its subgroup is a symmetry of the quiver description of theory
{\cal T}
. In the preceding subsection, we take
{\cal T}
to be as described by the affine
D_{4}
quiver (
3.1
) with
{\cal X}=\mathfrak{so}(8)
and
G
is a subgroup of
S_{4}
.

Let us first consider the case in which
G\cong\mathfrak{N}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{h}
, where
\mathfrak{N}
is a normal subgroup of
G
. For simplicity and definiteness of the discussion, we assume that
\mathfrak{N}
is a product of Abelian factors
\mathfrak{N}=\prod_{i}\mathbb{Z}_{s_{i}}
. We can refine the index
{\cal I}_{\cal T}
of theory
{\cal T}
with respect to the fugacities associated with the generators of
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
and each factor
\mathbb{Z}_{s_{i}}
in
\mathfrak{N}
. Here are the important points:

•

We observe that gauging
\mathfrak{N}
leads to the index of theory
{\cal T}
wreathed by
\Gamma_{w}
, namely
{\cal T}/\Gamma_{w}
, where
\Gamma_{w}
is such that

\begin{split}\mathbb{Z}_{h}\cong G/\Gamma_{w}\end{split}

(3.157)

where we have discussed the following examples in the precedent subsections:

\begin{split}&(G,\,\mathfrak{N},\,\mathbb{Z}_{h},\,\Gamma_{w})\\
&=\left\{(S_{3},\,\mathbb{Z}_{3},\,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\,\mathbb{Z}_{3})~,~(\mathrm{Dih}_{8},\,\mathbb{Z}_{4},\,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\,\mathrm{NK})~,~(A_{4},\,\mathrm{NK},\,\mathbb{Z}_{3},\,\mathrm{NK})\right\}~,\end{split}

(3.158)

where, as before,
NK
denotes the normal Klein subgroup of
S_{4}
. Note that, in the second case, the wreathing group
\Gamma_{w}=\text{NK}
does not coincide with the group that is gauged, namely
\mathfrak{N}=\mathbb{Z}_{4}
. The reason for the latter is due to a mixed ’t Hooft anomaly, as explained below (
3.104
).

We remark that gauging the symmetry
\mathfrak{N}
of theory
{\cal T}
leads to a split two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}\cong\prod_{i}\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{s_{i}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{h}
formed by the dual one-form symmetry
\prod_{i}\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{s_{i}}
and the remaining zero-form symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
. When
G
is a semi-direct product, the two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}
does not have an ’t Hooft anomaly and can be gauged; see e.g.
[
18
,
17
,
15
,
16
]
.

•

On the other hand, an attempt to directly gauge the quotient group
\mathbb{Z}_{h}\cong G/\mathfrak{N}
from the index
{\cal I}_{\cal T}
leads to the so-called
invalid
index. Such an index has certain undesirable properties. For example, upon taking the Higgs or Coulomb branch limit to obtain the Higgs or Coulomb branch Hilbert series, the result contains terms with negative coefficients. These are not reasonable since the Hilbert series counts gauge invariant operators on the Higgs or Coulomb branch. We have provided various reasons for this, for example, below (
3.87
) and the second bullet point on Page 47 for the case of
S_{3}
. We also observe that the invalid index is related to the fact that gauging
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
makes
G
become a non-invertible symmetry, whose fusion category is
\text{2-Rep}(\Gamma^{(2)})
; see e.g.
[
18
,
17
,
21
,
15
,
22
,
16
]
.

•

Such an invalid index can be “cured” as follows. Recall that theory
{\cal T}
with
\mathfrak{N}
gauged has a split two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}
which is anomaly-free. Now we can gauge the two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}
in this theory to obtain theory
{\cal T}
gauged by
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
, i.e.
{\cal T}/\mathbb{Z}_{h}
, via the relation

\begin{split}({\cal T}/\mathfrak{N})/\Gamma^{(2)}\cong{\cal T}/\mathbb{Z}_{h}~.\end{split}

(3.159)

The resulting index turns out to be a valid one, namely that of theory
{\cal T}
wreathed by
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
, as indicated by the right-hand side. In terms of the index, the gauging of the two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}
can be performed by first summing over the fugacities of
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
(as well as dividing by
h
), and then restoring an appropriate combination of fugacities of
\mathfrak{N}
.

Let us now suppose that theory
{\cal T}
has a zero-form discrete finite symmetry
G
which is a non-trivial extension of
\mathfrak{N}=\prod_{i}\mathbb{Z}_{s_{i}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
, with
\mathfrak{N}
a normal subgroup of
G
as before. In this case, upon gauging
\mathfrak{N}
, the resulting two-group
\Gamma^{(2)}
formed by the dual symmetry of
\mathfrak{N}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
has an ’t Hooft anomaly. Furthermore, let us suppose that the continuous symmetry
{\cal X}
of
{\cal T}
becomes
{\cal X}^{\prime}
which is a subalgebra of
{\cal X}
after gauging
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
. The symmetry
{\cal X}^{\prime}
is determined from the moment map operators that transform in the adjoint representation of
{\cal X}^{\prime}
. However, upon counting the marginal operators after gauging
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
, we see that such a number does not fit into the representations appearing in the branching rule of
{\cal X}
to
{\cal X}^{\prime}
. Moreover, we observe a certain universal result upon computing the difference between the index of theory
{\cal T}
with
\mathbb{Z}_{h}
gauged and that of the wreathed quiver with global symmetry
{\cal X}^{\prime}
. For the affine
D_{4}
quiver (
3.1
) for which
{\cal X}=\mathfrak{so}(8)
, we denote
{\cal X}^{\prime}
in such cases by
\mathfrak{su}(4)*
and
\mathfrak{su}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(1)**
. The corresponding indices are presented in Figures
3
and
6
, and such a universal result is reported in (
3.78
). It would be nice to understand the physical origin of this result in future work.

3.11
\mathbb{Z}_{6}
discrete gauging: a combination of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
wreathing and
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\omega}

Let us now discuss a
\mathbb{Z}_{6}
discrete gauging of theory (
3.1
) which is realised in a different fashion from those presented in the precedent subsections. Given the index (
3.87
), we can combine the fugacities
g
and
\omega
associated with the
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{g}
and the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\omega}
symmetries into a fugacity
t\equiv\omega g
associated with a
(\mathbb{Z}_{6}^{[0]})_{t}
symmetry. Written in terms of the fugacities
t
and
h
, the index (
3.87
) reads

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}}(a|t,h;x)\\
&=1+\left(6+4t+3t^{2}+8t^{3}+3t^{4}+4t^{5}\right)a^{-2}x\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+\left\{\left(t^{2}+t^{4}\right)a^{4}+\left[56+36t+41t^{2}+48t^{3}+41t^{4}+36t^{5}\right.\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.\left.+\left(6+8t+6t^{2}+8t^{3}+6t^{4}+8t^{5}\right)h\right]a^{-4}\right.\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.-\left[7+4t+3t^{2}+8t^{3}+3t^{4}+4t^{5}\right]\right\}x^{2}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(3.160)

from which we can gauge the
(\mathbb{Z}_{6}^{[0]})_{t}
symmetry and obtain the index of theory
(\ref{affineD4})/(\mathbb{Z}_{6}^{[0]})_{t}
:

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4}/(\mathbb{Z}_{6}^{[0]})_{t}}(a|h;x)=1+6a^{-2}x+\left[\left(56+6h\right)a^{-4}-7\right]x^{2}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.161)

The same index can be obtained equivalently starting from (
3.87
) and gauging the
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{g}
and the
(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[0]})_{\omega}
symmetries sequentially, as shown in Figure
8
. This discretely gauged theory will also be considered in the upcoming work
[
58
]
.

(\ref{affineD4})/(\mathbb{Z}_{6}^{[0]})_{t}

\mathfrak{su}(2)^{2}

1+6a^{-2}x+\left(62a^{-4}-7\right)x^{2}

\,\,\,\,+\left(a^{6}+a^{2}+341a^{-6}-123a^{-2}\right)x^{3}+\ldots

(\ref{affineD4})/\mathbb{Z}_{3}

\mathfrak{g}_{2}

\,\,\,\,+\left[\left(62+56\omega\right)a^{-4}-\left(7+8\omega\right)\right]x^{2}

\,\,\,\,+\left[a^{6}+a^{2}+\left(341+352\omega\right)a^{-6}-\left(123+120\omega\right)a^{-2}\right]x^{3}+\ldots

\mathfrak{su}(2)^{4}

\,\,\,\,+\left(a^{6}+949a^{-6}-a^{2}-329a^{-2}\right)x^{3}+\ldots

(
3.1
)

\,\,\,\,+\left[2a^{4}+\left(156+144\omega\right)a^{-4}-\left(13+16\omega\right)\right]x^{2}

\,\,\,\,+\left[a^{6}+\left(949+976\omega\right)a^{-6}-a^{2}-\left(329+320\omega\right)a^{-2}\right]x^{3}+\ldots

(\mathbb{Z}_{6}^{[0]})_{t}
(\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{[0]})_{g}

Figure 8
:
\mathbb{Z}_{6}
discrete gauging of the affine

and

4
Charge conjugation and flavour symmetry of SQCD

In this section, we consider gauging the charge conjugation symmetry associated with the flavour symmetry of the 3d
{\cal N}=4

\mathrm{U}(N)
gauge theory with
n
flavours.
21
21

21

We will refer to this theory as SQCD for general
N
and as SQED for
N=1
.
In the following, we study such gauging from two perspectives: the first one involves superconformal indices of the wreathed mirror theory of SQCD, and the second one involves the chiral ring and Hilbert series of the Higgs branch of SQED.

4.1

\U(N)
gauge theory with

flavours is

{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -82.68 -23.62 M -82.68 -23.62 C -81.43 -26.11 -78.53 -27.77 -74.38 -27.77 L -8.3 -27.77 C -4.15 -27.77 -1.25 -29.43 0 -31.92 C 1.25 -29.43 4.15 -27.77 8.3 -27.77 L 74.38 -27.77 C 78.53 -27.77 81.43 -26.11 82.68 -23.62}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-22.56357pt}{-33.1287pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -31.22 -45.84)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\end{split}

(4.1)

This quiver has a left-right symmetry with respect to the vertical axis. For
n
odd, this axis of symmetry passes through the bifundamental hypermultiplet in the middle of the quiver, whereas, for
n
even, it passes through the gauge node with label
N
in the middle of the quiver. The prescription for computing the index of quiver (
4.1
) wreathed by this
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry is therefore different in these two cases. We refer the reader to Appendix
A.1
for a detailed analysis. For simplicity, we focus on the cases of
N=1
and
N=2
. We report the results for
n
odd and
n
even separately below.

The cases with odd
n

Let us focus on the cases with odd
n
. As explained in Appendix
A.1
, quiver (
4.1
) with
n
odd wreathed by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
can be realised using (
A.48b
), (
A.51
), (
A.52
). The corresponding index can be obtained from equations (
A.61
)–(
A.68
). Their explicit expressions, up to order
x^{3}
, are given below.

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{c|l}\hline\cr$N=1$&\qquad\qquad\quad Index of the $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ wreathed (\ref{mirrUnwNflv})\\
\hline\cr$n=3$&$1+\left(a^{2}+{5}{a^{-2}}\right)x+a^{3}x^{3/2}+\left(a^{4}+{15}{a^{-4}}-4\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+\left(a^{5}-a\right)x^{5/2}+\left(2a^{6}+{34}{a^{-6}}-{22}{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$n=5$&$1+\left(a^{2}+{14}{a^{-2}}\right)x+\left(a^{4}+{105}{a^{-4}}-11\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+a^{5}x^{5/2}+\left(a^{6}+{510}{a^{-6}}-{221}{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$n=7$&$1+\left(a^{2}+{27}{a^{-2}}\right)x+\left(a^{4}+{378}{a^{-4}}-22\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+\left(a^{6}+{3164}{a^{-6}}-{897}{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+a^{7}x^{7/2}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr$N=2$&\qquad\qquad\quad Index of the $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ wreathed (\ref{mirrUnwNflv})\\
\hline\cr$n=3$&Equal to as that of $N=1$ and $n=3$\\
\hline\cr$n=5$&$1+\left(a^{2}+{14}{a^{-2}}\right)x+a^{3}x^{3/2}+\left(2a^{4}+{145}{a^{-4}}+3\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+\left(2a^{5}+23a\right)x^{5/2}+\left(4a^{6}+3a^{2}+{1030}{a^{-6}}-{175}{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$n=7$&$1+\left(a^{2}+{27}{a^{-2}}\right)x+\left(2a^{4}+{581}{a^{-4}}+5\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+a^{5}x^{5/2}+\left(2a^{6}+5a^{2}+{8316}{a^{-6}}-{756}{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(4.2)

It is interesting to consider the Coulomb branch moment maps, which contribute to the term
a^{-2}x
in the index. The coefficients
5
,
14
, and
27
that appear above are precisely the dimensions of the principal extension
\widetilde{\mathrm{SU}}(n)_{\text{I}}=\mathrm{SU}(n)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
of
\mathrm{SU}(n)

[
39
,
40
,
41
]
with the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
charge conjugation symmetry being gauged . Let us explain this statement as follows. According to
(
41
, Table 2)
, such a principal extension has a representation which is known as
\mathrm{Adj}\otimes\chi
, whose character is
22
22

22

To be consistent with the rest of the paper, we denote by
\chi
the fugacity associated with the charge conjugation symmetry. Note, however, that in
[
41
]
this is denoted by
\epsilon
.

\begin{split}\chi^{\mathrm{SU}(n)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}}_{\mathrm{Adj}\otimes\chi}(\bm{z},\chi)&=\left(\frac{1+\chi}{2}\right)\chi^{\mathrm{SU}(n)}_{\text{Adj}}(\bm{z})-(1-n)\left(\frac{1-\chi}{2}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{1+\chi}{2}\right)\left[(n-1)+\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\frac{z_{i}}{z_{j}}\right]-(1-n)\left(\frac{1-\chi}{2}\right)~.\end{split}

(4.3)

Observe that upon setting
\chi=1
, we indeed obtain the character
\chi^{\mathrm{SU}(n)}_{\text{Adj}}(\bm{z})
of the adjoint representation of
\mathrm{SU}(n)
. On the other hand, we can gauge the charge conjugation symmetry by summing over
\chi=\pm 1
and obtain the following character
23
23

23

Note that we can perform an analogous computation for
\mathrm{SO}(n)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, where the character of the adjoint representation is given by
(
41
, (2.11))
. Upon gauging the charge conjugation symmetry, we obtain the character of the adjoint representation of
\USp(2n-2)
.

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\chi=\pm 1}\chi^{\mathrm{SU}(n)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}}_{\mathrm{Adj}\otimes\chi}(\bm{z},\chi)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\chi^{\mathrm{SU}(n)}_{\text{Adj}}(\bm{z})+n-1\right)~.\end{split}

(4.4)

Setting
z_{i}=1
, we obtain the dimension of the corresponding representation

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)=\frac{1}{2}(n-1)(n+2)~.\end{split}

(4.5)

This is precisely the coefficient of the term
a^{-2}x
that appears in the index. In other words, this confirms that wreathing of the mirror theory (
4.1
) for
n
odd indeed reproduces gauging of the charge conjugation symmetry associated with the flavour symmetry of the
\U(N)
gauge theory with
n
flavours.
24
24

24

For the special case of
N=1
and
n=3
, we find that the coefficients of the
a^{-2p}x^{p}
terms in the index is given by
\frac{1}{2}(p+1)\left(p^{2}+2p+2\right)
. These terms consitute the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of the wreathed mirror theory (
4.1
), or equivalently the Higgs branch Hilbert series of SQED with three flavours with the charge conjugation symmetry associated with the flavour symmetry ibeing gauged. Such a coefficient can also be written as
\dim\,[p,p]_{\mathfrak{su}(3)}-3\dim\,[p-1,0]_{\mathfrak{usp}(4)}
.

Let us examine the Higgs branch of the wreathed mirror theory (
4.1
). The corresponding Hilbert series can be constructed from the coefficients
c_{p}
of the terms
a^{2p}x^{p}
in the index, namely
\sum_{p\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}}c_{p}t^{2p}
. For
N=1
, we find that the explicit expression of the Higgs branch Hilbert series of the wreathed theory in question is

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{2}+t^{n}]~.\end{split}

(4.6)

It is instructive to compare this with the Higgs branch Hilbert series of the original quiver (
4.1
) without wreathing, or equivalently the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of the
\U(1)
gauge theory (SQED) with
n
flavours, namely

\begin{split}\mathrm{HS}[\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}](t,w)=\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{2}+(w+w^{-1})t^{n}-t^{2n}]~.\end{split}

(4.7)

We see that upon gauging the charge conjugation symmetry associated with the flavour symmetry of SQED, precisely one of the elementary monopole operators is projected out. As a result, the relation that contributes to the term
t^{2n}
in the Hilbert series also disappears. We observe a similar phenomenon for the case of
N=2
.
25
25

25

As an example, for
N=2
and
n=5
, we find that the Hilbert series in question is
\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{2}+t^{3}+t^{4}+t^{5}+t^{6}-t^{16}]
, whereas the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of
\U(2)
SQCD with five flavours is
\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+(w+w^{-1})t^{3}+t^{4}+(w+w^{-1})t^{5}-t^{8}-t^{10}\right]
.

The cases with even
n

Let us now turn to the cases with even
n
. As reported in Appendix
A.1
, quiver (
4.1
) with
n
even wreathed by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
can be obtained from (
A.48a
), (
A.49
), (
A.50
). The corresponding index can be derived using equations (
A.53
)–(
A.60
). Explicitly, the expressions of the indices up to order
x^{3}
are given below.

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{c|l}\hline\cr$N=1$&\qquad Index of the $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ wreathed (\ref{mirrUnwNflv})\\
\hline\cr$n=4$&$1+{10}{a^{-2}}x+\left(2a^{4}+{49}{a^{-4}}-11\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+\left(a^{6}+{165}{a^{-6}}-a^{2}-{93}{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$n=6$&$1+{21}{a^{-2}}x+\left(a^{4}+{216}{a^{-4}}-22\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+\left(a^{6}+{1386}{a^{-6}}-{495}{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr$N=2$&\qquad Index of the $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ wreathed (\ref{mirrUnwNflv})\\
\hline\cr$n=4$&$1+\left(3a^{2}+{10}{a^{-2}}\right)x+\left(6a^{4}+{64}{a^{-4}}+16\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+\left(10a^{6}+12a^{2}+{270}{a^{-6}}-{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$n=6$&$1+{21}{a^{-2}}x+\left(3a^{4}+{321}{a^{-4}}-8\right)x^{2}$\\
&$\,\,\,\,+\left(2a^{6}+40a^{2}+{3276}{a^{-6}}-{446}{a^{-2}}\right)x^{3}+\ldots$\\
\end{tabular}\end{split}

(4.8)

Observe that the coefficient of the term
a^{-2}x
, which is the contribution of the Coulomb branch moment map, is the dimension of the adjoint representation
\USp(n)
. This means that the Coulomb branch symmetry of the wreathed mirror theory (
4.1
) is
\USp(n)
.

Let us consider the case of
N=1
, namely SQED with
n
flavours. Wreathing quiver (
4.1
) gives the mirror dual to the
\mathrm{O}(2)
gauge theory with
n/2
flavours:

(4.9)

where it can be checked that the indices of both theories are equal upon transforming
a\leftrightarrow a^{-1}
. The Higgs branch of (

) is the closure of next to the minimal nilpotent orbit of
\mathfrak{usp}(n)
, namely
\overline{\text{n.min}\,C_{n/2}}
, and the Coulomb branch of (
4.9
) is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{n}
. We see that the Hilbert series of these are equal to the Coulomb branch and Higgs branch limits of the index given by the coefficients of the terms
a^{-2p}x^{p}
and
a^{2p}x^{p}
, respectively. We see that in this case wreathing is equivalent to gauging the charge conjugation symmetry that turns the
\U(1)\cong\mathrm{SO}(2)
gauge group of SQED into the
\mathrm{O}(2)
gauge group. Moreover, it is worth discussing the case of

and

in detail. Note that SQED with four flavours is dual to the

gauge theory with three flavours:

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}{}}{{}{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -73.57 0 L -5.17 0 M 5.17 0 L 71.94 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
