\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\right)_{p}$}\end{split}

(A.81)

where each round bracket with a subscript
m
contains the nodes belonging to the
m
-th set, with
m=1,\ldots,p
.

Let us deal with the adjoint chiral multiplets first, whose contribution is encoded in the matrix

\begin{split}\Phi=\left(\begin{array}[]{c c c| c c c| c| c c c}\Phi_{b_{1}}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\Phi_{b_{u}}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\hline\cr\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\Phi_{c_{1}}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\Phi_{c_{v}}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\hline\cr\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\hline\cr\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\Phi_{d_{1}}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\Phi_{d_{r}}\\
\end{array}\right)~,\end{split}

(A.82)

which generalises (
A.29
), where the matrices
\Phi_{A}
, with
A=[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]
, are defined as (
A.28
) by substituting
p_{i,l}
with
y_{i,A}
. We can then act on
\Phi
with the matrix representation of element
[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]
, defined in (
A.76
), and obtain the following contribution to the Higgs branch Hilbert series:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.94}{$\begin{split}&\mathop{\rm det}{\left(\mathds{1}-\gamma_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}\Phi t^{2}\right)}\\
&=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[-\left\{\sum_{i,j=1}^{Y}y_{i,1}y_{j,1}^{-1}\right\}t^{2u}-\left\{\sum_{i,j=1}^{Y}y_{i,2}y_{j,2}^{-1}\right\}t^{2v}-\ldots-\left\{\sum_{i,j=1}^{Y}y_{i,p}y_{j,p}^{-1}\right\}t^{2r}\right]~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(A.83)

We recognise (
A.83
) to be the contribution coming from
p

\U(Y)
adjoint chiral fields, whose associated gauge fugacities
y_{i,1},y_{i,2},\ldots,y_{i,p}
are defined as in (
A.78
), with
t^{2}
rescaled into
t^{2m}
, with
m=u,v,r,\ldots
, meaning that we have to send
(x,a)
into
(x^{m},a^{m})
.

Finally, let us study the action of element
[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]
on the Haar measure for the
\U(Y)
gauge groups in quiver (
A.71
). Let us take the matrices
\mu_{A}
, which are defined as (
A.39
) by substituting
y_{i,l}
with
y_{i,A}
, and combine them into a single matrix
\mu
, which generalises (
A.40
), given by

\begin{split}\mu=\left(\begin{array}[]{c c c| c c c| c| c c c}\mu_{b_{1}}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mu_{b_{u}}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\hline\cr\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mu_{c_{1}}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mu_{c_{v}}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\hline\cr\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\hline\cr\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mu_{d_{1}}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mymathds{0}&\ldots&\mu_{d_{r}}\\
\end{array}\right)~.\end{split}

(A.84)

If we consider the determinant

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathop{\rm det}{\left(\mathds{1}-\gamma_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}\mu\right)}=\prod\limits_{i<j}^{Y}\left(1-y_{i,1}y_{j,1}^{-1}\right)\left(1-y_{i,2}y_{j,2}^{-1}\right)\times\ldots\times\left(1-y_{i,p}y_{j,p}^{-1}\right)~,$}\end{split}

(A.85)

we observe that this coincides with the contribution entering in the Haar measure of
p

\U(Y)
gauge groups, with gauge fugacities
y_{i,1},y_{i,2},\ldots,y_{i,p}
.

Taking into account both (
A.83
) and (
A.85
), it follows that
(\ref{ngaugenodes})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}
can be depicted as

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{81.95059pt}{-12.93553pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 113.4 -17.9)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

(A.86)

where we highlight in
red
,
blue
and
violet
the gauge fugacities and the rescaling of the fugacities
x
and
a
associated with the gauge contributions coming from the
first
,
second
and
p
-th
set of nodes in (
A.81
) respectively.
32
32

32

Recall that the adjoint chiral belonging to the 3d
{\cal N}=4
vector multiplet has charge
2
under the
\U(1)
axial symmetry, see Footnote
30
. Hence, if we send
a\rightarrow a^{m}
, the adjoint chirals in (
A.86
) are refined with fugacity
a^{-2m}
.
The associated index reads

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref{ngaugenodes})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}(w_{y,1},n_{y,1}|w_{y,2},n_{y,2}|\ldots|w_{y,p},n_{y,p}|a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\frac{1}{(Y!)^{p}}\sum_{(\bm{m}^{y}_{1},\bm{m}^{y}_{2},\ldots,\bm{m}^{y}_{p})\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}}\oint\left(\prod_{j=1}^{Y}\frac{dy_{j,1}}{2\pi iy_{j,1}}y_{j,1}^{n_{y,1}}w_{y,1}^{m^{y}_{j,1}}\right){\cal Z}^{\U(Y)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{y}_{1};\bm{m}^{y}_{1};x^{u}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\times\left(\prod_{j=1}^{Y}\frac{dy_{j,2}}{2\pi iy_{j,2}}y_{j,2}^{n_{y,2}}w_{y,2}^{m^{y}_{j,2}}\right){\cal Z}^{\U(Y)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{y}_{2};\bm{m}^{y}_{2};x^{v}\right)\times\ldots\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\times\left(\prod_{j=1}^{Y}\frac{dy_{j,p}}{2\pi iy_{j,p}}y_{j,p}^{n_{y,p}}w_{y,p}^{m^{y}_{j,p}}\right){\cal Z}^{\U(Y)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{y}_{p};\bm{m}^{y}_{p};x^{r}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{i,j=1}^{Y}{\cal Z}^{1}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,1}y_{j,1}^{-1}a^{-2u};m^{y}_{i,1}-m^{y}_{j,1}-2un_{a};x^{u}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{i,j=1}^{Y}{\cal Z}^{1}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,2}y_{j,2}^{-1}a^{-2v};m^{y}_{i,2}-m^{y}_{j,2}-2vn_{a};x^{v}\right)\times\ldots\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{i,j=1}^{Y}{\cal Z}^{1}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,p}y_{j,p}^{-1}a^{-2r};m^{y}_{i,p}-m^{y}_{j,p}-2rn_{a};x^{r}\right)~,\end{split}

(A.87)

where
(w_{y,1},n_{y,1}),(w_{y,2},n_{y,2}),\ldots,(w_{y,p},n_{y,p})
are the fugacities and background fluxes associated with the topological symmetries of the
p

\U(Y)
gauge nodes appearing in (
A.86
). In particular, if we denote with
w_{j,m}
the topological fugacities of the
\U(Y)
nodes in (
A.81
), with
m=1,\ldots,p
and
j=1,\ldots l(m)\equiv
number of
\U(Y)
nodes in the
m
-th set, then we define
w_{y,m}\equiv\prod_{j=1}^{l(m)}w_{j,m}
.

On the other hand, the 3d
{\cal N}=4
vector multiplet associated with the
\U(Z)
node in (
A.71
) is not affected by the action of the element
[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]
and its contribution to the index is the usual one for a vector multiplet, namely it is given by (
A.47
).

In conclusion, taking into account (
A.79
) and (
A.86
), we find that the contribution coming from the action of the group element
[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]
on the star-shaped quiver (
A.71
) can be depicted as

(A.88)

where the wreathed quiver
(\ref{starshapedQ})/H
can be obtained from equation (
A.72
) by summing over all possible contributions of the form (
A.88
) corresponding to elements of
H
. Using (
A.47
), (
A.80
) and (
A.87
), the index of (
A.88
) reads

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.97}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref{starshapedQg})}={\cal I}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}(w_{y,1},n_{y,1}|w_{y,2},n_{y,2}|\ldots|w_{y,p},n_{y,p}|w_{z},n_{z}|a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\frac{1}{(Y!)^{p}Z!}\sum_{(\bm{m}^{y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{m}^{y}_{p})\in\mathbb{Z}^{pY}}\,\sum_{\bm{m}^{z}\in\mathbb{Z}^{Z}}{\cal I}^{\text{vec}}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}\,\,{\cal I}^{\text{adj}}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}\,\,{\cal I}^{\text{chir}}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(A.89)

where

\begin{split}{\cal I}^{\text{vec}}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}=&\left(\prod_{j=1}^{Y}\frac{dy_{j,1}}{2\pi iy_{j,1}}y_{j,1}^{n_{y,1}}w_{y,1}^{\bm{m}^{y}_{j,1}}\right){\cal Z}^{\U(Y)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{y}_{1};\bm{m}^{y}_{1};x^{u}\right)\\
\times&\left(\prod_{j=1}^{Y}\frac{dy_{j,2}}{2\pi iy_{j,2}}y_{j,2}^{n_{y,2}}w_{y,2}^{\bm{m}^{y}_{j,2}}\right){\cal Z}^{\U(Y)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{y}_{2};\bm{m}^{y}_{2};x^{v}\right)\times\ldots\\
\times&\left(\prod_{j=1}^{Y}\frac{dy_{j,p}}{2\pi iy_{j,p}}y_{j,p}^{n_{y,p}}w_{y,p}^{\bm{m}^{y}_{j,p}}\right){\cal Z}^{\U(Y)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{y}_{p};\bm{m}^{y}_{p};x^{r}\right)\\
\times&\left(\prod_{j=1}^{Z}\frac{dz_{j}}{2\pi iz_{j}}z_{j}^{n_{z}}w_{z}^{\bm{m}^{z}_{j}}\right){\cal Z}^{\U(Z)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{z};\bm{m}^{z};x\right)\times\ldots~,\end{split}

(A.90)

\begin{split}{\cal I}^{\text{adj}}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}=&\prod_{i,j=1}^{Y}{\cal Z}^{1}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,1}y_{j,1}^{-1}a^{-2u};m^{y}_{i,1}-m^{y}_{j,1}-2un_{a};x^{u}\right)\\
\times&\prod_{i,j=1}^{Y}{\cal Z}^{1}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,2}y_{j,2}^{-1}a^{-2v};m^{y}_{i,2}-m^{y}_{j,2}-2vn_{a};x^{v}\right)\times\ldots\\
\times&\prod_{i,j=1}^{Y}{\cal Z}^{1}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,p}y_{j,p}^{-1}a^{-2r};m^{y}_{i,p}-m^{y}_{j,p}-2rn_{a};x^{r}\right)\\
\times&\prod_{i,j=1}^{Z}{\cal Z}^{1}_{\chi}\left(z_{i}z_{j}^{-1}a^{-2};m^{z}_{i}-m^{z}_{j}-2n_{a};x\right)\times\ldots~,\end{split}

(A.91)

\begin{split}{\cal I}^{\text{chir}}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}=&\prod_{i=1}^{Y}\prod_{j=1}^{Z}\prod_{s=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,1}^{s}z_{j}^{-us}a^{u};sm^{y}_{i,1}-sm^{z}_{j}+un_{a};x^{u}\right)\\
\times&\prod_{i=1}^{Y}\prod_{j=1}^{Z}\prod_{s=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,2}^{s}z_{j}^{-vs}a^{v};sm^{y}_{i,2}-sm^{z}_{j}+vn_{a};x^{v}\right)\times\ldots\\
\times&\prod_{i=1}^{Y}\prod_{j=1}^{Z}\prod_{s=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}\left(y_{i,p}^{s}z_{j}^{-rs}a^{r};sm^{y}_{i,p}-sm^{z}_{j}+rn_{a};x^{r}\right)\times\ldots~.\end{split}

(A.92)

In case there are special unitary nodes, we have to set the corresponding fugacities and magnetic fluxes as specified in (
A.69
). Upon setting the background magnetic fluxes associated with the topological symmetries to zero, the index of the wreathed quiver
(\ref{starshapedQ})/H
can then be obtained by summing over the indices of all possible contributions corresponding to elements of
H
and dividing by the order of
H
:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})/H}(\bm{w}|w_{z}|a;x)\\
&=\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]\in H}{\cal I}_{(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}}(w_{y,1}=w_{b_{i}}^{l(1)}|w_{y,2}=w_{c_{j}}^{l(2)}|\ldots|w_{y,p}=w_{d_{k}}^{l(p)}|w_{z}|a;x)~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(A.93)

where we denote with
l(m)
the length of the
m
-th cycle appearing in the group element
[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]
, with
m=1,\ldots,p
, and with
b_{i},c_{j},\ldots,d_{k}
generic entries of the
m
-th cycle of such element, as specified by (
A.73
). In order to obtain a well-defined index, we have to set
w_{m}=w_{m^{\prime}}
whenever, throughout the sum over all the elements of
H
, two different group elements act on the same
\U(Y)
node of quiver (
A.71
) via two distinct cycles
m
and
m^{\prime}
, namely the same
\U(Y)
node appears in two different sets labelled by subscripts
m
and
m^{\prime}
in (
A.81
).

33

For the sake of explicitness, let us take
H=S_{3}
and
n=4
in quiver (
A.71
). We can redefine the topological fugacities associated to each contribution
(\ref{starshapedQ})_{[b^{u},c^{v},\ldots,d^{r}]}
participating in the sum over all elements of
S_{3}
as follows:

\begin{split}\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathds{1}\equiv(1)(2)(3)(4)&:\quad w_{y,1}=w_{1},w_{y,2}=w_{2},w_{y,3}=w_{3},w_{y,4}=w_{4}~,\\
(12)\equiv(12)(3)(4)&:\quad w_{y,1}=w_{1}^{2},w_{y,2}=w_{3},w_{y,3}=w_{4}~,\\
(13)\equiv(13)(2)(4)&:\quad w_{y,1}=w_{1}^{2},w_{y,2}=w_{2},w_{y,3}=w_{4}~,\\
(23)\equiv(23)(1)(4)&:\quad w_{y,1}=w_{2}^{2},w_{y,2}=w_{1},w_{y,3}=w_{4}~,\\
(123)\equiv(123)(4)&:\quad w_{y,1}=w_{1}^{3},w_{y,2}=w_{4}~,\\
(132)\equiv(132)(4)&:\quad w_{y,1}=w_{1}^{3},w_{y,2}=w_{4},~.\end{array}\end{split}

(A.94)

Since the node
\U(Y)_{2}
associated with the fugacity
w_{2}
appears both in the first set and in the second set corresponding to the first and second cycles, we have to set
w_{2}=w_{1}
. A similar consideration holds also for the node
\U(Y)_{3}
associated with the fugacity
w_{3}
, meaning that we also have to set
w_{3}=w_{1}
.
This observation generalises the comment below (
A.70b
) and ensures that the series expansion of the index does not contain half-integer coefficients, which might otherwise arise due to the presence of the overall factor
1/|H|
.
